  165: # _is_safe_url($url) -> bool
  166: #
  167: # Purpose: SSRF guard.  Blocks the most dangerous classes of Server-Side Request
  168: #          Forgery targets: loopback aliases (localhost, 127/8) and literal
  169: #          private/link-local/CGNAT IPv4 addresses in the URL host component.
  170: #
  171: # Design rationale for scope:
  172: #   Resolving hostnames via DNS and checking the result is ineffective: a
  173: #   separate DNS lookup happens at LWP connect time (TOCTOU / DNS rebinding
  174: #   window).  The authoritative defence against hostname-based SSRF is a
  175: #   network-layer egress firewall.  This function handles the Perl-layer
  176: #   interception for the most common patterns that operators cannot easily
  177: #   filter at the network level: bare loopback aliases and literal private IPs
  178: #   hard-coded by an attacker.
  179: #
  180: # Blocked targets:
  181: #   localhost / 127.0.0.0/8 / 0.0.0.0 / ::1  -- loopback aliases
  182: #   10.0.0.0/8    -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  183: #   172.16.0.0/12 -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  184: #   192.168.0.0/16-- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  185: #   169.254.0.0/16-- link-local; AWS/GCP/Azure metadata endpoint (literal IP)
  186: #   100.64.0.0/10 -- CGNAT / Tailscale shared space (literal IP)
  187: #
  188: # Hostname-based targets (e.g. http://internal.corp.example.com/) are allowed
  189: # at this layer; block them with egress firewall rules instead.
  190: sub _is_safe_url {
  191: 	my $url = $_[0];
  192: 
  193: 	return 0 unless $url =~ m{\Ahttps?://([^/:?\[\]#]+)}i;

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  194: 	my $host = lc $1;
  195: 
  196: 	# Block well-known loopback aliases.
  197: 	return 0 if $host eq 'localhost'

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  198: 	          || $host =~ /\A127\./
  199: 	          || $host eq '0.0.0.0'
  200: 	          || $host eq '::1';
  201: 
  202: 	# For literal IPv4 addresses only: check private/link-local/CGNAT ranges.
  203: 	# Skipping DNS for hostname targets avoids a live network call in tests and
  204: 	# removes the TOCTOU window that makes DNS-resolved checks illusory anyway.
  205: 	return 1 unless $host =~ /\A\d{1,3}(?:\.\d{1,3}){3}\z/;

  218: # _safe_back_url($url) -> $url | undef
  219: #
  220: # Purpose: Sanitise a user-supplied back-link URL before it is passed to the
  221: #          template and rendered inside an href="" attribute.  Template Toolkit's
  222: #          | html filter encodes <>&" but does NOT block the javascript: or
  223: #          data: URI schemes, so a raw param('back') = "javascript:alert(1)"
  224: #          would be rendered verbatim inside the href and execute on click.
  225: #

  227: #          are permitted.  Anything else (javascript:, data:, vbscript:, empty
  228: #          strings, undef) returns undef so the template renders no href.
  229: #
  230: sub _safe_back_url {
  231: 	my ($url) = @_;
  232: 	return undef unless defined $url && length($url);

  264: # _detect_platform($user_agent, $fallback) -> $platform_string
  265: #

  279: 	};

  459: # _apply_filter_spec($records, $spec) -> \@filtered_records
  460: #
  461: # Purpose: Apply one "col:op:val" filter to an arrayref of record hashrefs and
  462: #          return a new (possibly shorter) arrayref.  The original is not mutated.
  463: #          Value may contain colons: "col:op:val:with:colons" -- split limit 3
  464: #          puts everything after the second colon into $val.
  465: #          Unknown operators fall through to 1 (all rows pass) so new operators
  466: #          added in future do not break existing callers.
  467: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $spec is a colon-delimited string.
  468: # Exit:    Returns filtered arrayref (same reference if spec is invalid/unparseable).
  469: #
  470: # Domain Constraints (operator names):
  471: #   Operator names are CASE-SENSITIVE.  Only the exact lowercase forms below are
  472: #   recognised; an uppercase or mixed-case name (e.g. "EQ", "Lt") falls through
  473: #   to the default `1` branch and all rows pass unfiltered.
  474: #   Valid operators:
  475: #     String (value comparison is case-insensitive via lc()):
  476: #       eq, ne, contains, starts
  477: #     Numeric (Perl <, <=, >, >= coercion):
  478: #       lt (strict), le (inclusive), gt (strict), ge (inclusive)
  479: #     Unary (val is ignored):
  480: #       empty (cell eq ''), notempty (cell ne '')
  481: #   Boundary rules for numeric operators:
  482: #     le/ge include the boundary value; lt/gt exclude it.
  483: #   contains/starts with empty val ('') match every record (index always 0).
  484: #
  485: # Reduction: split(/:/, $spec, 3) always produces at least one defined element,
  486: # so "defined $col" is structurally guaranteed true and is removed.
  487: # "$_->{$col} // ''" replaces the equivalent ternary form.
  488: #
  489: # Optimisation: $lval = lc($val) is computed once before the grep.  Without this
  490: # precompute, every case-insensitive operator (eq, ne, contains, starts) would
  491: # call lc($val) O(N) times for N records -- a pure O(N) waste since $val is
  492: # constant within a single filter application.
  493: sub _apply_filter_spec {
  494: 	my ($records, $spec) = @_;
  495: 	my ($col, $op, $val) = split /:/, $spec, 3;
  496: 	return $records unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  497: 	$val //= '';
  498: 	my $lval = lc $val;	# precompute once; avoids O(N) redundant lc() inside grep
  499: 	return [grep {
  500: 		my $cell = $_->{$col} // '';
  501: 		$op eq 'eq'       ? lc($cell) eq $lval                :
  502: 		$op eq 'ne'       ? lc($cell) ne $lval                :
  503: 		$op eq 'contains' ? index(lc($cell), $lval) != -1     :

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  504: 		$op eq 'starts'   ? index(lc($cell), $lval) == 0      :

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  505: 		$op eq 'lt'       ? $cell <  $val                     :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  506: 		$op eq 'le'       ? $cell <= $val                     :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  507: 		$op eq 'gt'       ? $cell >  $val                     :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  508: 		$op eq 'ge'       ? $cell >= $val                     :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  509: 		$op eq 'empty'    ? $cell eq ''                        :
  510: 		$op eq 'notempty' ? $cell ne ''                        :
  511: 		1
  512: 	} @$records];
  513: }

  560: # _filter_specs_to_criteria( \@specs, $source ) -> ( \%criteria, \@residual_specs )
  561: #
  562: # Purpose: Map the f= filter vocabulary onto Database::Abstraction / Database::Join
  563: #          criteria hashrefs so the backend can apply pushable filters before
  564: #          paginating.  Specs that cannot be safely pushed are returned as
  565: #          @residual_specs for subsequent in-memory application.
  566: #
  567: # Pushdown is only performed when $source->dbi_source() confirms a SQL (SQLite)
  568: # backend.  For CSV (slurp path), in-memory, XLSX, BerkeleyDB and URL sources
  569: # everything is placed in @residual_specs so the existing _apply_filter_spec
  570: # Perl path handles them.
  571: #
  572: # Pushable mapping (SQLite only):
  573: #   eq / ne         -- LIKE / NOT LIKE without wildcards.  SQLite LIKE is
  574: #                      case-insensitive for ASCII, matching the existing Perl
  575: #                      lc() semantics.  Values containing % or _ are pushed to
  576: #                      residual to avoid LIKE metacharacter conflicts.
  577: #   contains        -- LIKE '%val%'.  Residual when value contains % or _.
  578: #   starts          -- LIKE 'val%'.   Residual when value contains % or _.
  579: #   lt / le / gt / ge -- SQL numeric comparison.  Residual when the value is
  580: #                      not a plain number (avoids column-affinity surprises
  581: #                      with currency or comma-formatted values).
  582: #   empty / notempty -- Always residual.  Must match both SQL NULL and the
  583: #                      literal empty string; cannot be expressed as a single
  584: #                      D::A criterion without an -or grouping.
  585: #
  586: # Column collision: if a column already has a pushable criterion, the second
  587: # spec for that column is placed in @residual_specs.  This keeps the AND logic
  588: # simple and safe; callers with multiple conditions on one column (e.g. a range)
  589: # still get correct results via the in-memory fallback.

  745: # _grouped_totals($self, \%pipeline, \%spec) -> \@rows
  746: #
  747: # Single GROUP BY entry point for all four chart routes.  Returns an arrayref
  748: # of hashrefs, each containing the 'by' column values and the aggregate result
  749: # under $spec->{as}.
  750: #

  752: #   source  -- a DataSource or Database::Join for the push path; undef when

  754: #   records -- the already-fetched rows (pre-filtered by the caller).

  756: # $spec = { by => [...], fn => ..., val => ..., as => ..., [sort_by, having, limit, offset] }
  757: #
  758: # Dispatch:

  760: #                 _measure_pushable), and either no active f= filters or all
  761: #                 active filters are SQL-pushable (via _filter_specs_to_criteria).

  910: # _fetch_page( $self, $source, \%params ) -> ( $records, $has_next )
  911: #   params: per_page  -- page size (required)
  912: #           offset    -- 0-based row offset (required)
  913: #           dedup     -- truthy when ?d=1 dedup is requested
  914: #           sort_col  -- column name to sort by (optional)
  915: #           sort_dir  -- 'asc' or 'desc' (default 'asc'; ignored unless sort_col set)
  916: #
  917: # Purpose: Fetch and reduce one page of records from $source, honouring the
  918: #          reduce-before-paginate ordering contract.  Two modes:
  919: #
  920: #   Fast path (no f= filters, no dedup): uses the N+1 trick
  921: #     selectall_arrayref(limit => per_page+1, offset => offset).  has_next is
  922: #     determined by the extra row before it is popped.
  923: #
  924: #   Reduction path (f= filters or dedup present): materialises the full result
  925: #     in memory, applies all f= filters with _apply_filter_spec (case-insensitive),
  926: #     applies dedup if requested, then paginates in Perl.  This guarantees that
  927: #     the Nth page contains the Nth page of the *reduced* set, not the Nth page
  928: #     of the raw set reduced to fewer rows.  Memory cost: the full reduced set.
  929: #
  930: # Sort: sort_col/sort_dir are forwarded to selectall_arrayref as
  931: #   sort_by => [$sort_col, $sort_dir].  Both Database::Abstraction 0.49+
  932: #   and Database::Join 0.009.0 honour this parameter natively.
  933: #
  934: # Returns (undef, 0) when the underlying fetch throws; the caller must handle
  935: # $records being undef.

 2701: # _serialize_csv($records, \@columns) -> $utf8_string
 2702: #
 2703: # Purpose: Build a complete RFC 4180 CSV document (header + data rows) from
 2704: #          $records.  Shared by _render_csv (HTTP download) and the csv branch
 2705: #          of export_write (filesystem write) to eliminate duplicated logic.
 2706: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $columns is an arrayref of names.
 2707: # Exit:    Returns a UTF-8 Perl string ending with CRLF.
 2708: #
 2709: # Optimisation: rows are collected in @lines and joined in one operation.
 2710: # The previous pattern ($out .= _csv_row(...) per row) triggers O(N) string
 2711: # reallocations; joining a pre-built array is a single O(total_bytes) allocation.
 2712: # For exports of thousands of rows the difference is measurable.
 2713: #
 2714: # XS note: for very large exports, replacing _csv_row with Text::CSV_XS
 2715: # (available from CPAN) would further reduce serialisation time -- its C-level
 2716: # implementation is roughly 5-10x faster than the pure-Perl version here.
 2717: sub _serialize_csv {
●2718 → 2720 → 2723 2718: 	my ($records, $columns) = @_;
 2719: 	my @lines = _csv_row(@$columns);
 2720: 	for my $row (@$records) {
 2721: 		push @lines, _csv_row(map { $row->{$_} } @$columns);
 2722: 	}
 2723: 	return encode('UTF-8', join('', @lines));

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2724: }

 2817: =head2 view
 2818: 
 2819: C<GET /view/:table> -- Open and display the chosen table from C<data_dir>.
 2820: 
 2821: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2822: 
 2823: =head4 INPUT
 2824: 
 2825:   :table   string   Table name (alphanumeric + underscore).  Returns 404 on
 2826:                     other characters.
 2827:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 2828: 
 2829: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: :table
 2830: 
 2831: C<:table> is validated against C<\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z> before C<open_table>
 2832: is ever called.  The first character must be a letter (A-Z, a-z) or an
 2833: underscore; subsequent characters may also include digits (0-9).
 2834: 
 2835: =over 4
 2836: 
 2837: =item Valid partition
 2838: 
 2839: C<sales> (letters only), C<_temp> (underscore-start), C<report_2024>
 2840: (mixed).  A name that is valid but has no backing file returns 200 with
 2841: an error message -- NOT 404.
 2842: 
 2843: =item Invalid partition
 2844: 
 2845: C<1sales> (digit-start, 404), C<my.data> (dot, 404), C<my-data>
 2846: (hyphen, 404), non-ASCII characters (404).
 2847: 
 2848: =item Boundary values
 2849: 
 2850: C<a> (single letter, valid), C<_> (single underscore, valid), C<1>
 2851: (single digit, 404), C<a1> (letter then digit, valid), C<1a> (digit
 2852: then letter, 404).
 2853: 
 2854: =back
 2855: 
 2856: =head4 OUTPUT
 2857: 
 2858: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with table data.

 2874: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2875: 
 2876:   GET /view/sales      -> 200 HTML table of all sales rows

 3047: 
 3048: =head2 open_file
 3049: 
 3050: C<GET /open> -- Open a supported data file from any absolute filesystem path.
 3051: 
 3052: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3053: 
 3054: =head4 INPUT
 3055: 
 3056:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to the data file.  Returns 404 when missing,
 3057:                     not a regular file, or the extension is not in SUPPORTED_EXT.
 3058:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3059: 
 3060: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?path=
 3061: 
 3062: =over 4
 3063: 
 3064: =item Extension filter (C<EXT_RE>)
 3065: 
 3066: The basename must match C<\.(?:csv|db|sql|xml|tsv|psv)\z> (case-insensitive).
 3067: The C<\z> anchor (absolute end-of-string) means a URL-encoded trailing
 3068: newline (e.g. C<file.csv%0A> decoded to C<file.csv\n>) does NOT pass --
 3069: the C<\n> falls after the C<\z> boundary and the extension check fails.
 3070: 
 3071: =item Valid partition
 3072: 
 3073: C</data/sales.csv> (lowercase extension), C</tmp/REPORT.CSV> (uppercase
 3074: extension, /i matches), any C<.db>, C<.sql>, C<.xml>, C<.tsv>, C<.psv> regular
 3075: file.
 3076: 
 3077: =item Invalid partition
 3078: 
 3079: Absent C<?path=> (404), non-existent file (404), directory instead of
 3080: file (404), unsupported extension such as C<.txt> (404), empty string
 3081: (404), path containing a C<%0A> (newline) suffix (404).
 3082: 
 3083: =item Double-extension filenames
 3084: 
 3085: A file like C<file.php.csv> passes C<EXT_RE> (last segment is C<.csv>)
 3086: but produces table stem C<file.php> which fails C<DataSource>'s
 3087: C<TABLE_NAME_RE>.  The C<open_table> call is inside C<eval>, so the
 3088: croak is caught and the action returns 200 with a friendly error, not
 3089: a 500.
 3090: 
 3091: =back
 3092: 
 3093: =head4 OUTPUT
 3094: 
 3095: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with C<back_url> pointing to the
 3096: directory's C</browse> page, and C<file_path> set to the resolved absolute
 3097: path (used by the template to record the file in C<localStorage>).
 3098: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with C<error>, C<back_url>, C<back_label>.
 3099: 
 3100: =head3 MESSAGES
 3101: 
 3102:   error_file_open   -- DataSource threw during initialisation or fetch_all.
 3103: 
 3104: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3105: 
 3106:   open_file == lambda self .
 3107:     let file = realpath(param('path')) in
 3108:     pre  is_file(file) /\ basename(file) =~ EXT_RE
 3109:     let src = open_table(stem(file), dir=dirname(file)) in

 3348: =head2 import_url
 3349: 
 3350: C<GET /import> -- Fetch a remote HTML page, extract the first (or selected)
 3351: table, and render it as a sortable data grid.
 3352: 
 3353: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3354: 
 3355: =head4 INPUT
 3356: 
 3357:   ?url=          string   Required.  Full http:// or https:// URL of the page
 3358:                           that contains the HTML table.  Returns home with an
 3359:                           error message if the scheme is wrong, the fetch fails,
 3360:                           or no table is found at the specified index.
 3361:   ?table_index=  integer  Zero-based index of the C<< <table> >> to use when
 3362:                           the page contains more than one table (default: 0).
 3363:   ?f=            string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3364: 
 3365: =head4 OUTPUT
 3366: 
 3367: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the same stash shape as C<open_file>,
 3368: plus C<source_url> (the original URL) for C<localStorage> tracking.
 3369: 
 3370: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with an C<error> stash variable.
 3371: 
 3372: =head3 MESSAGES
 3373: 
 3374:   error_url_required  -- empty or missing C<url> param
 3375:   error_url_invalid   -- URL does not begin with http:// or https://
 3376:   error_url_fetch     -- LWP fetch failure or no table found at the index
 3377: 
 3378: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3379: 
 3380:   GET /import?url=https://matrix.perl-magpie.org/dist/Database-Abstraction/
 3381:   GET /import?url=https://example.com/data.html&table_index=2
 3382: 
 3383: =cut
 3384:

 3450: =head2 columns_api
 3451: 
 3452: C<GET /api/columns> -- Return column names for a table or file as JSON.
 3453: 
 3454: Used by the join UI to populate the right-key dropdown without a page reload.
 3455: 
 3456: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3457: 
 3458: =head4 INPUT
 3459: 
 3460:   ?table=  string   Table name from C<data_dir>.
 3461:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to a data file.
 3462:   ?spec=   string   Unified spec: C<"table:name"> or C<"path:/abs/path">.
 3463:                     Parsed only when C<table> and C<path> are both absent.
 3464: 
 3465: One of C<table>, C<path>, or C<spec> must be present; C<table> takes
 3466: precedence over C<path>, and both take precedence over C<spec>.
 3467: 
 3468: =head4 OUTPUT
 3469: 
 3470:   200 application/json   { "columns": ["col1", "col2", ...] }
 3471:   404 application/json   { "error": "not found" }
 3472: 
 3473: =head3 MESSAGES
 3474: 
 3475: None produced by this action directly; internal errors yield a 404.
 3476: 
 3477: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3478: 
 3479:   columns_api == lambda self .
 3480:     let src = open_spec(table_param or path_param or parse_spec(spec_param)) in
 3481:     pre  src /= undef
 3482:     post render_json({ columns: get_columns(src) })
 3483: 
 3484: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3485: 
 3486:   GET /api/columns?table=sales            -> {"columns":["product","region","amount"]}
 3487:   GET /api/columns?spec=table:sales       -> {"columns":["product","region","amount"]}
 3488:   GET /api/columns?spec=path:/data/f.csv  -> {"columns":[...]}
 3489:   GET /api/columns?table=noexist          -> 404
 3490: 
 3491: =cut
 3492:

 3543: =head2 join_tables
 3544: 
 3545: C<GET /join> -- Perform one or more left joins and render the merged table.
 3546: 
 3547: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3548: 
 3549: =head4 INPUT
 3550: 
 3551:   ?l=    string   Required. Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".
 3552:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 3553:   ?j=    string   (repeatable) Join step: "<right-spec>|<left-key>|<right-key>".
 3554:                   Invalid or non-existent steps are silently skipped.
 3555:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 3556: 
 3557: =head4 OUTPUT
 3558: 
 3559: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the merged/filtered result set.
 3560: 
 3561: =head3 MESSAGES
 3562: 
 3563:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 3564: 
 3565: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3566: 
 3567:   join_tables == lambda self .
 3568:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 3569:     pre  left /= undef
 3570:     let joined  = fold(_left_join, left, param_list('j')) in
 3571:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, joined, param_list('f')) in
 3572:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 3573: 
 3574: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 3575: 
 3576:   1. Parse and open left table spec; 404 on failure.
 3577:   2. For each j= param (in order):
 3578:        a. Parse right-spec, left-key, right-key from "|"-split.
 3579:        b. Verify left-key exists in current column list.
 3580:        c. Open right table; skip step on any error.
 3581:        d. Verify right-key exists in right column list.
 3582:        e. Call _left_join; update records and columns.
 3583:   3. Apply all f= filters via _apply_filter_spec.
 3584:   4. Build stable table key for localStorage ("join:left:right1:...").
 3585:   5. Render dashboard.
 3586: 
 3587: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3588: 
 3589:   GET /join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 3590:     -> merged left join of sales and products on product/name columns
 3591: 
 3592: =cut
 3593:

 3707: 
 3708: =head2 combine_tables
 3709: 
 3710: C<GET /combine> -- Stack rows from two or more tables vertically into a single
 3711: unified view.
 3712: 
 3713: Unlike C</join> (which appends columns from a matching row in a second table),
 3714: C</combine> appends the I<rows> from a second table beneath the rows of the
 3715: first.  All columns from all sources appear as headers; where a row has no
 3716: value for a particular column (because that column did not exist in its source
 3717: file) the cell is left blank.
 3718: 
 3719: This is equivalent to a SQL C<UNION ALL> across heterogeneous schemas.
 3720: 
 3721: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3722: 
 3723: =head4 INPUT
 3724: 
 3725:   ?l=    string   (required) Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".
 3726:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 3727:   ?c=    string   (repeatable) Additional table spec to stack beneath the
 3728:                   current result.  Invalid or non-existent specs are silently
 3729:                   skipped.
 3730:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 3731: 
 3732: =head4 OUTPUT
 3733: 
 3734: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the combined result set.
 3735: 
 3736: =head3 MESSAGES
 3737: 
 3738:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 3739: 
 3740: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3741: 
 3742:   combine_tables == lambda self .
 3743:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 3744:     pre  left /= undef
 3745:     let sources  = [left] ++ map(open_spec, param_list('c')) in
 3746:     let combined = _combine_tables(sources) in
 3747:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, combined, param_list('f')) in
 3748:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 3749: 
 3750: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3751: 
 3752:   GET /combine?l=table:cats&c=table:dogs
 3753:     -> unified view: all cat and dog rows; Species/Name/Color/Breed/Eye Color
 3754:        appear for both; Environment (cats-only) and Sex/Fixed (dogs-only)
 3755:        are blank where the source file lacks that column.
 3756: 
 3757: =cut
 3758:

 3864:

 3866: 
 3867: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3868: 
 3869: =head4 INPUT
 3870: 
 3871:   ?l=        string   (required) Left table spec.
 3872:   ?j=        string   (repeatable) Join steps.
 3873:   ?f=        string   (repeatable) Filter specs.
 3874:   ?format=   string   "csv" (default), "sqlite", or "json".
 3875: 
 3876: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?format=
 3877: 
 3878: Exact lowercase string match.
 3879: 
 3880: =over 4
 3881: 
 3882: =item Valid partitions
 3883: 
 3884: C<csv> (explicit CSV), C<sqlite> (SQLite binary), C<json> (JSON array),
 3885: absent/undef (defaults to CSV).
 3886: 
 3887: =item Invalid partitions (all fall back to CSV)

 3890:

 3894:

 3913: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3914: 
 3915:   GET /export?l=table:sales&format=csv     -> downloads sales.csv

 3942: =head2 export_write
 3943: 
 3944: C<POST /export> -- Write the current logical view to a chosen filesystem path.
 3945: 
 3946: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3947: 
 3948: =head4 INPUT
 3949: 
 3950:   l=         string   (required) Left table spec.
 3951:   j=         string   (repeatable) Join steps.
 3952:   f=         string   (repeatable) Filter specs.
 3953:   dir=       string   Target directory (must exist; resolved via realpath).
 3954:   filename=  string   Output filename including extension.  Extension determines
 3955:                       format: C<.csv> -> RFC 4180 CSV; C<.sql> -> SQLite;
 3956:                       C<.json> -> JSON array of row objects.
 3957: 
 3958: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: filename=
 3959: 
 3960: The extension check uses C</\.csv\z/i> (CSV), C</\.sql\z/i> (SQLite), or
 3961: C</\.json\z/i> (JSON): the C</i> flag makes matching case-insensitive, so
 3962: C<.CSV>, C<.SQL>, and C<.JSON> are accepted alongside lowercase forms.
 3963: Everything else returns 415.
 3964: 
 3965: Path separator characters (C</> and C<\>) in C<filename> are stripped
 3966: first via C<m{([^/\\]+)\z}> -- only the basename is kept, preventing
 3967: directory traversal.
 3968: 
 3969: =over 4
 3970: 
 3971: =item Valid partitions
 3972: 
 3973: C<report.csv>, C<report.sql>, C<report.json>, C<REPORT.CSV>, C<report.SQL>.
 3974: A single-character stem (C<a.csv>) is also valid.
 3975: 
 3976: =item Invalid partitions (415)
 3977: 
 3978: C<report.txt>, C<report> (no extension), empty string.
 3979: 
 3980: =back
 3981: 
 3982: =head4 OUTPUT
 3983: 
 3984:   200 application/json   { "saved": "/abs/path/to/file" }
 3985:   404 application/json   { "error": "..." }  -- bad dir or table not found
 3986:   415 application/json   { "error": "..." }  -- unsupported extension
 3987:   500 application/json   { "error": "..." }  -- write failure
 3988: 
 3989: =head3 MESSAGES
 3990: 
 3991:   error_dir_not_found   -- realpath of dir failed or result is not a directory.
 3992:   error_ext_required    -- filename extension is not .csv or .sql.
 3993:   error_write_failed    -- DBI or filesystem write threw.
 3994: 
 3995: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3996: 
 3997:   export_write == lambda self .
 3998:     let dir  = realpath(param('dir'))      in
 3999:     let file = dir / strip_path(param('filename')) in
 4000:     pre  is_dir(dir) /\ ext(file) in {csv, sql}
 4001:     let (recs, cols, _) = _run_export_pipeline() in
 4002:     pre  recs /= undef
 4003:     post spurt(file, serialise(recs, cols))
 4004:          /\ render_json({ saved: file.to_string })

 4118: =head2 stat_api
 4119: 
 4120: C<GET /api/stat> -- Return filesystem metadata for a file path.
 4121: 
 4122: Used by the home page tooltip to show modification time and size for recently
 4123: opened files without a page reload.
 4124: 
 4125: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4126: 
 4127: =head4 INPUT
 4128: 
 4129:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to query.  Returns HTTP 400 when absent.
 4130: 
 4131: =head4 OUTPUT
 4132: 
 4133:   200 application/json   (file exists)
 4134:     { "exists": true, "path": "/resolved/path", "mtime": 1700000000, "size": 4096 }
 4135: 
 4136:   200 application/json   (file does not exist)
 4137:     { "exists": false, "path": "/original/path" }
 4138: 
 4139:   400 application/json   { "error": "\"path\" parameter is required" }
 4140: 
 4141: C<mtime> is Unix epoch seconds.  C<size> is bytes.  A missing/unresolvable
 4142: path returns HTTP 200 with C<exists: false> (not 404) so the UI can
 4143: distinguish "file was deleted" from a request error.
 4144: 
 4145: =head3 MESSAGES
 4146: 
 4147:   error_path_required   -- the "path" query parameter was not supplied.
 4148: 
 4149: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4150: 
 4151:   stat_api == lambda self .
 4152:     let path = param('path') in
 4153:     pre  path /= undef
 4154:     let file = realpath(path) in
 4155:     post if is_file(file) then
 4156:            render_json({ exists: true, mtime: mtime(file), size: size(file) })
 4157:          else render_json({ exists: false })
 4158: 
 4159: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4160: 
 4161:   GET /api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv
 4162:     -> { "exists": true, "path": "/data/sales.csv", "mtime": 1700000000, "size": 1234 }
 4163: 
 4164:   GET /api/stat?path=/deleted.csv
 4165:     -> { "exists": false, "path": "/deleted.csv" }
 4166: 
 4167: =cut

 4215: =head2 upload_file
 4216: 
 4217: C<POST /upload> -- Accept a drag-and-dropped data file and return a redirect URL.
 4218: 
 4219: The file is saved under its original filename in a managed subdirectory of
 4220: C<< <app_home>/.uploads/ >>.  The subdirectory name is randomised so that
 4221: concurrent uploads of files with the same name do not collide.
 4222: 
 4223: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4224: 
 4225: =head4 INPUT
 4226: 
 4227: Multipart form upload, field name: C<file>.
 4228: 
 4229:   file   upload   Supported extensions: csv, db, sql, xml, tsv, psv.
 4230: 
 4231: =head4 OUTPUT
 4232: 
 4233:   200 application/json   { "url": "/open?path=/abs/path/file.csv", "path": "/abs/path/file.csv" }
 4234:   400 application/json   { "error": "No file received" }
 4235:   413 application/json   { "error": "File too large to upload (limit: 50 MB)" }
 4236:   415 application/json   { "error": "Unsupported file type. Accepted: ..." }
 4237:   500 application/json   { "error": "Could not save uploaded file" }
 4238: 
 4239: =head3 MESSAGES
 4240: 
 4241:   error_upload_none       -- no file was received in the multipart upload.
 4242:   error_upload_ext        -- the file's extension is not in the supported list.
 4243:   error_upload_too_large  -- the upload body exceeded MAX_UPLOAD_MIB (50 MiB).
 4244:   error_upload_save       -- move_to failed or the destination file was not
 4245:                              created (I/O error, full disk, permission error).
 4246: 
 4247: =head3 IMPLEMENTATION NOTES
 4248: 
 4249: The C<move_to> call is wrapped with the C<eval { ...; 1 }> idiom (not
 4250: C<eval {}> + C<if ($@)>).  Mojolicious dispatch and DESTROY-phase evals can
 4251: leave C<$@> set before the action runs; checking C<if ($@)> after a new eval
 4252: may fire falsely on that stale value.  C<eval { move_to(); 1 }> returns 1 on
 4253: success and C<undef> on exception, so the result is independent of any prior
 4254: C<$@> value.
 4255: 
 4256: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4257: 
 4258:   upload_file == lambda self .
 4259:     let upload = req.upload('file') in
 4260:     pre  upload /= undef /\ basename(upload.filename) =~ EXT_RE
 4261:          /\ NOT req->is_limit_exceeded
 4262:          /\ upload.size <= MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES
 4263:     let dest = home/.uploads/<random>/<filename> in
 4264:     let ok = eval { upload.move_to(dest); 1 } in
 4265:     pre  ok /\ -f dest
 4266:     post render_json({ url: '/open?path=' ++ url_escape(dest), path: dest })
 4267: 
 4268: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4269: 
 4270:   POST /upload  (multipart: file=@sales.csv)
 4271:     -> { "url": "/open?path=%2F...%2Fsales.csv", "path": "/.../.uploads/abc123/sales.csv" }
 4272: 
 4273: =cut
 4274:

 4687: 
 4688: =head2 heatmap_view
 4689: 
 4690: C<GET /heatmap> -- Render a D3.js grid heatmap for two categorical axes.
 4691: 
 4692: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 4693: 
 4694:   x=<col>      X-axis (horizontal) categorical column (required)
 4695:   y=<col>      Y-axis (vertical) categorical column (required)
 4696:   val=<col>    Cell value column (optional; when absent, cells show row count)
 4697:   scheme=<name> D3 sequential colour scheme name (default: YlOrRd)
 4698:   show_val=1   Overlay the numeric value inside each cell
 4699:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 4700: 
 4701: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4702: 
 4703: =head4 INPUT
 4704: 
 4705:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 4706:   x       string   Required.  Name of the X-axis categorical column.
 4707:   y       string   Required.  Name of the Y-axis categorical column.
 4708:   val     string   Optional.  Name of the value column; omit for count mode.
 4709:   scheme  string   Optional.  D3 sequential colour scheme name.
 4710:   show_val flag    Optional.  Set to 1 to render values inside cells.
 4711:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 4712: 
 4713: =head4 OUTPUT
 4714: 
 4715:   200 text/html           Rendered heatmap page.
 4716:   200 text/plain          "No plottable data: ..." when grid is empty.
 4717:   400 text/plain          Missing x= or y= parameter.
 4718:   400 text/plain          Column not found: <col>.
 4719:   404 text/plain          "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 4720: 
 4721: =head3 MESSAGES
 4722: 
 4723:   error_heatmap_no_data -- no (x,y) pair has a non-empty value.
 4724: 
 4725: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4726: 
 4727:   heatmap_view == lambda self .
 4728:     pre length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 4729:         /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 4730:     let triples = { (row[x_col], row[y_col], row[val_col]) | row in records,
 4731:                     all three fields non-empty } in
 4732:     post render(heatmap, triples)
 4733: 
 4734: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4735: 
 4736:   GET /heatmap?l=table:sales&x=region&y=product&val=amount
 4737:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 heatmap SVG
 4738: 
 4739: =cut
 4740:

 4855: =head2 bar_view
 4856: 
 4857: C<GET /bar> -- Render a D3.js bar chart grouped by a categorical column.
 4858: 
 4859: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 4860: 
 4861:   cat=<col>    Category (X-axis) column (required)
 4862:   val=<col>    Value column to sum per category; omit or use C<__count__>
 4863:                to count rows instead of summing (optional)
 4864:   orient=h     Horizontal bars (default: vertical)
 4865:   sort=value|label  Sort bars by descending value or ascending label
 4866:   max=N        Collapse bars beyond the top-N into an "Other" bar
 4867:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 4868: 
 4869: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 4870: 
 4871: =head4 INPUT
 4872: 
 4873:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 4874:   cat     string   Required.  Name of the category column.
 4875:   val     string   Optional.  Name of the value column; use __count__ or
 4876:                    omit entirely to count rows per category.
 4877:   orient  string   Optional.  "h" for horizontal bars; default vertical.
 4878:   sort    string   Optional.  "value" or "label"; default file order.
 4879:   max     integer  Optional.  Collapse beyond top-N categories into "Other".
 4880:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 4881: 
 4882: =head4 OUTPUT
 4883: 
 4884:   200 text/html           Rendered bar chart page (SVG element id="bar_chart").
 4885:   200 text/plain          "No plottable data: ..." when no categories exist.
 4886:   400 text/plain          Missing cat= parameter.
 4887:   400 text/plain          Column not found: <col>.
 4888:   404 text/plain          "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 4889: 
 4890: =head3 MESSAGES
 4891: 
 4892:   error_bar_no_data -- no row has a non-empty category value.
 4893: 
 4894: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 4895: 
 4896:   bar_view == lambda self .
 4897:     pre length(cat_col) > 0 /\ cat_col in columns
 4898:     let bars = { (cat, sum(val)) | row in records, row[cat_col] = cat,
 4899:                  val /= undef } in
 4900:     post render(bar, bars, orientation, sort, max_bars)
 4901: 
 4902: =head3 EXAMPLE
 4903: 
 4904:   GET /bar?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&sort=value
 4905:     -> 200 HTML page with id="bar_chart" SVG element
 4906: 
 4907: =cut

 5029: =head2 folder_view
 5030: 
 5031: C<GET /folder> -- Browse a data table grouped by the letter-prefix hierarchy
 5032: of an order-number column (e.g. A10, CT4, B3).
 5033: 
 5034: The top level displays one folder icon per initial letter group (A, B, C ...).
 5035: Clicking a folder with sub-prefixes (e.g. C contains CA and CB items) shows
 5036: the sub-folders; clicking a leaf prefix shows the actual data rows sorted
 5037: numerically by the embedded digit sequence.
 5038: 
 5039: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 5040: 
 5041:   col=<col>      Column whose values contain order codes (optional; auto-
 5042:                  detected when absent by scanning the first 10 rows for
 5043:                  values matching C<[A-Z]{1,4}\d+[A-Z]?>)
 5044:   prefix=<str>   Current drill-down prefix, e.g. "CA" (optional; top-level
 5045:                  when absent)
 5046:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional;
 5047:                  derived from C<l=> when absent)
 5048: 
 5049: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 5050: 
 5051: =head4 INPUT
 5052: 
 5053:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 5054:   col     string   Optional.  Order-code column name.
 5055:   prefix  string   Optional.  Current letter prefix; max length MAX_PREFIX_LEN.
 5056:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 5057: 
 5058: =head4 OUTPUT
 5059: 
 5060:   200 text/html    Rendered folder page with folder grid and/or item table.
 5061:   400 text/plain   "Missing l= parameter" when l= is absent.
 5062:   400 text/plain   "Prefix too long" when prefix exceeds MAX_PREFIX_LEN (20).
 5063:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when the data source is unavailable.
 5064: 
 5065: =head3 MESSAGES
 5066: 
 5067: None -- all failure paths render plain text with an HTTP status code.
 5068: 
 5069: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5070: 
 5071:   folder_view == lambda self .
 5072:     pre length(left_spec) > 0 /\ length(prefix) <= MAX_PREFIX_LEN
 5073:     let records = pipeline(left_spec, joins, filters)
 5074:         prefix_map = { p -> [row] | row in records, row[col] =~ /[A-Z]+/ -> p } in
 5075:     IF prefix = "" THEN
 5076:       post render(folder, folders = first_letters(prefix_map))
 5077:     ELSE
 5078:       post render(folder, folders = sub_prefixes(prefix, prefix_map),
 5079:                           items   = sort_numeric(prefix_map[prefix]))
 5080: 
 5081: =head3 EXAMPLE
 5082: 
 5083:   GET /folder?l=table:orders&col=order_no
 5084:     -> 200 HTML folder grid showing A (12 items), B (7 items), C (4 items)
 5085: 
 5086:   GET /folder?l=table:orders&col=order_no&prefix=A
 5087:     -> 200 HTML folder grid for sub-prefixes and items directly under "A"
 5088: 
 5089: =cut
 5090:

 6621: 
 6622: Database::BI::Controller::Dashboard - Home picker, filesystem browser, table
 6623: viewer, left-join engine, result filter, export, and file upload
 6624: 
 6625: =head1 SYNOPSIS
 6626: 
 6627: All routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.  You do not
 6628: call its methods directly -- Mojolicious dispatches HTTP requests to them
 6629: automatically.  The examples below show browser URLs and their curl
 6630: equivalents.
 6631: 
 6632: B<Open the home page and see all tables in the data directory:>
 6633: 
 6634:   # Browser
 6635:   http://localhost:3000/
 6636: 
 6637:   # curl
 6638:   curl http://localhost:3000/
 6639: 
 6640: B<View a single table (file: data/sales.csv):>
 6641: 
 6642:   # Browser
 6643:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 6644: 
 6645:   # curl
 6646:   curl http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 6647: 
 6648: B<Filter rows -- show only rows where region equals "North":>
 6649: 
 6650:   # Browser (add ?f=col:op:val to any view URL)
 6651:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North
 6652: 
 6653:   # Multiple filters -- "North" AND amount greater than 100:
 6654:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North&f=amount:gt:100
 6655: 
 6656:   # curl
 6657:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North'
 6658: 
 6659: B<Join two tables -- left-join sales with products on the product column:>
 6660: 
 6661:   # Browser
 6662:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 6663: 
 6664:   # The j= parameter is: right-table-spec | left-key | right-key
 6665:   # You can chain multiple joins:
 6666:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&j=table:regions|region|id
 6667: 
 6668: B<Open a file anywhere on the filesystem (not just in data/):>
 6669: 
 6670:   http://localhost:3000/open?path=/home/user/reports/q3.csv
 6671: 
 6672: B<Download the current view as a CSV file:>
 6673: 
 6674:   # Uses the same l=, j=, f= parameters as /join
 6675:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=csv
 6676: 
 6677:   # Download as a SQLite database file instead:
 6678:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=sqlite
 6679: 
 6680:   # Download a filtered + joined result:
 6681:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&f=region:eq:North&format=csv
 6682: 
 6683: B<Save the current view to a file on the server (instead of downloading):>
 6684: 
 6685:   # POST with form fields; format is inferred from the filename extension
 6686:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 6687:        -F l=table:sales \
 6688:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 6689:        -F filename=report.csv
 6690: 
 6691:   # Save as SQLite:
 6692:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 6693:        -F l=table:sales \
 6694:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 6695:        -F filename=report.sql
 6696: 
 6697: B<Get the column list for a table (used by the join UI):>
 6698: 
 6699:   curl http://localhost:3000/api/columns?table=sales
 6700:   # Returns: {"columns":["product","region","amount","date"]}
 6701: 
 6702: B<Check when a file was last modified (used by the tooltip on the home page):>
 6703: 
 6704:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv'
 6705:   # Returns: {"exists":true,"path":"/data/sales.csv","mtime":1700000000,"size":1234}
 6706: 
 6707: B<Browse the filesystem to find a data file:>
 6708: 
 6709:   http://localhost:3000/browse
 6710:   http://localhost:3000/browse?path=/home/user/data
 6711: 
 6712: B<Upload a data file by dropping it onto the page (multipart form POST):>
 6713: 
 6714:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/upload \
 6715:        -F file=@/home/user/data/sales.csv
 6716:   # Returns: {"url":"/open?path=/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv","path":"/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv"}
 6717: 
 6718: =head2 graph_view
 6719: 
 6720: C<GET /graph> -- Render a D3.js v7 line chart from the current data pipeline.
 6721: 
 6722: Accepts the same pipeline parameters as L</join_tables> (C<l=>, C<j=>,
 6723: C<c=>, C<f=>, C<d=>) plus:
 6724: 
 6725: =over 4
 6726: 
 6727: =item C<x=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 6728: 
 6729: Column name to use as the X axis (required).  Any data type is accepted;
 6730: values are displayed as categorical labels.
 6731: 
 6732: =item C<y=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 6733: 
 6734: Column name to use as the Y axis (required).  Non-numeric characters
 6735: (currency symbols, commas, etc.) are stripped before comparison; rows
 6736: where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are silently skipped.
 6737: 
 6738: =item C<back=E<lt>urlE<gt>>
 6739: 
 6740: URL for the "Back to table" link rendered in the graph toolbar.  Defaults
 6741: to C</> when absent.  The dashboard JS sets this to the current page URL
 6742: before navigating.
 6743: 
 6744: =back
 6745: 
 6746: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6747: 
 6748: =head4 INPUT
 6749: 
 6750:   l=<spec>     string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 6751:   x=<col>      string   X-axis column name (required).
 6752:   y=<col>      string   Y-axis column name (required; must be numeric).
 6753:   back=<url>   string   Back-link URL (optional; default "/").
 6754:   j=, c=, f=, d=        Pipeline params -- see L</join_tables>.
 6755: 
 6756: =head4 OUTPUT
 6757: 
 6758:   200 text/html   TT-rendered graph page.  The HTML::D3 snippet embeds a
 6759:                   C<const data = [...]> JSON array where each element has:
 6760:                     { label: <x-value>, value: <y-value>,
 6761:                       extra: { <col>: <val>, ... } }
 6762:                   C<extra> contains every column not used as X or Y, so
 6763:                   the hover tooltip shows the complete row for each point.
 6764:                   The chart supports brush-to-zoom; a "Reset zoom" button
 6765:                   appears after a brush selection.  The toolbar shows the
 6766:                   count of plotted data points.
 6767: 
 6768:   400 text/plain  C<x=> or C<y=> absent, or named column not found.
 6769:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 6770: 
 6771: =head3 MESSAGES
 6772: 
 6773:   Missing x or y column parameter          C<x=> or C<y=> query param absent.
 6774:   Column not found: <name>                  Named column absent from result set.
 6775:   Could not open data source                Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 6776:   No plottable data: no rows have valid ... All rows lack a numeric Y value.
 6777: 
 6778: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6779: 
 6780:   GET /graph?l=table:sales&x=product&y=amount
 6781: 
 6782: Renders a line chart of C<amount> (Y) against C<product> (X).  Mousing over
 6783: a dot shows a tooltip with the product name, the amount, and every other
 6784: column in that row (e.g. C<region>, C<date>).
 6785: 
 6786: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6787: 
 6788:   graph_view : Controller Ã— Params â†’ HTML | Error
 6789: 
 6790:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, c, f, d)
 6791:   let pairs    = [ [row[x], num(row[y]), row \ {x,y}]
 6792:                    | row âˆˆ pipeline.records,  num(row[y]) â‰  âŠ¥ ]
 6793: 
 6794:   pre  x âˆˆ params âˆ§ y âˆˆ params           else 400
 6795:   pre  x âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 6796:   pre  y âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 6797:   pre  pairs â‰  []                          else 200 "No plottable data"
 6798:   post HTML::D3->render_line_chart_snippet(pairs) embedded in TT layout
 6799: 
 6800: =cut
 6801: 
 6802: =head2 pie_view
 6803: 
 6804: C<GET /pie> -- Render a D3.js v7 animated pie chart from the current data
 6805: pipeline.
 6806: 
 6807: Each row's category value (C<cat=>) is used as a slice label; the numeric
 6808: value column (C<val=>) is summed per category.  Accounting-notation
 6809: negatives such as C<(1,234.56)> are handled automatically.  Slices are
 6810: sorted by total descending; when there are more than 12 distinct categories
 6811: the smallest ones are collapsed into a single "Other" slice.
 6812: 
 6813: Clicking any slice or legend entry navigates back to the table view with
 6814: an C<f=cat:eq:value> filter applied automatically, so the user can drill
 6815: into the data behind a slice without leaving the application.
 6816: 
 6817: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6818: 
 6819: =head4 INPUT
 6820: 
 6821:   l=<spec>    string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 6822:   cat=<col>   string   Category column name (required; used as slice labels).
 6823:   val=<col>   string   Numeric value column name (required; values are summed).
 6824:   donut=1     flag     Render as a donut chart with a hole in the centre (optional).
 6825:   back=<url>  string   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional; default "/").
 6826:   f=<spec>    string   Result filter (repeatable; same syntax as /join).
 6827: 
 6828: =head4 OUTPUT
 6829: 
 6830:   200 text/html   TT-rendered pie chart page containing:
 6831:                   - Two-level breadcrumb (Home > Back to table).
 6832:                   - Export buttons for SVG and PNG (legend is composited in).
 6833:                   - An animated D3.js pie or donut chart with a colour-coded
 6834:                     legend.  Each slice and legend entry is clickable and
 6835:                     navigates to the filtered table view.
 6836:                   - Slice count (e.g. "7 slices").
 6837: 
 6838:   200 text/plain  "No plottable data" when every row has a non-numeric or
 6839:                   missing value column (HTTP 200, not 4xx, so the page can
 6840:                   render a friendly message).
 6841: 
 6842:   400 text/plain  C<cat=> or C<val=> absent, or named column not found.
 6843:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 6844: 
 6845: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS
 6846: 
 6847:   cat=    Any column name that exists in the result set.  Cell values are
 6848:           used verbatim as slice labels.  Empty-string cells are skipped.
 6849: 
 6850:   val=    Must be a column whose cells contain numbers (after stripping
 6851:           currency symbols, commas, and handling accounting-notation negatives).
 6852:           Rows where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are skipped.
 6853:           Negative totals are included in the chart (slice values may be negative
 6854:           if the source data contains credits or reversals).
 6855: 
 6856:   donut=  Any truthy value (e.g. "1") enables the donut hole.  Absent or
 6857:           "0" renders a solid pie.
 6858: 
 6859: =head3 MESSAGES
 6860: 
 6861:   Missing cat or val column parameter    C<cat=> or C<val=> query param absent.
 6862:   Column not found: <name>               Named column absent from the result set.
 6863:   Could not open data source             Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 6864:   No plottable data: ...                 All rows lack a valid numeric value.
 6865: 
 6866: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6867: 
 6868:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&back=/view/sales
 6869: 
 6870: Renders a pie chart of total C<amount> per C<region>.  Clicking the
 6871: "North" slice navigates to C</view/sales?f=region:eq:North>.
 6872: 
 6873: With a donut hole:
 6874: 
 6875:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&donut=1
 6876: 
 6877: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6878: 
 6879:   pie_view : Controller x Params -> HTML | Error
 6880: 
 6881:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, f, d)
 6882:   let totals   = { cat => sum { val(row) | row in pipeline.records,
 6883:                                            cat(row) != '', num(val(row)) != bot }
 6884:                  | cat in range(cat_col) }
 6885: 
 6886:   pre  cat in params /\ val in params     else 400
 6887:   pre  cat in pipeline.cols               else 400
 6888:   pre  val in pipeline.cols               else 400
 6889:   pre  totals != {}                        else 200 "No plottable data"
 6890:   post HTML::D3->render_pie_chart_snippet(slices) embedded in TT layout
 6891:        where slices = sort_desc { |s.value| | s in totals }
 6892: 
 6893: =cut
 6894: 
 6895: =head2 clear_uploads
 6896: 
 6897: C<POST /uploads/clear> -- Delete every file from the C<.uploads/> staging
 6898: directory and return the total disk space recovered.
 6899: 
 6900: The C<.uploads/> directory accumulates files from drag-and-drop uploads.
 6901: It grows indefinitely; this action lets users reclaim that space without
 6902: needing shell access.
 6903: 
 6904: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6905: 
 6906: =head4 INPUT
 6907: 
 6908: No parameters.  The C<.uploads/> path is always the one under C<app->home>.
 6909: 
 6910: =head4 OUTPUT
 6911: 
 6912:   200 application/json   { "freed": <bytes>, "count": <n> }
 6913: 
 6914: C<freed> is the total number of bytes removed.  C<count> is the number of
 6915: files deleted.  Both are 0 when the directory is absent or already empty.
 6916: 
 6917: =cut
 6918: 
 6919: =head2 scatter_view
 6920: 
 6921: C<GET /scatter> -- Render a D3.js scatter plot or bubble chart.
 6922: 
 6923: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6924: 
 6925:   x=<col>        X-axis column (required; numeric)
 6926:   y=<col>        Y-axis column (required; numeric)
 6927:   series=<col>   Colour-by category column (optional)
 6928:   size=<col>     Bubble-radius numeric column (optional)
 6929:   scheme=<name>  D3 colour scheme (default tableau10; only used with series=)
 6930:   min_r=N        Minimum bubble radius in px (default 3; range 1-40)
 6931:   max_r=N        Maximum bubble radius in px (default 15; range 1-40)
 6932:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6933: 
 6934: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6935: 
 6936: =head4 INPUT
 6937: 
 6938:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 6939:   x        string  Required.  Numeric X-axis column name.
 6940:   y        string  Required.  Numeric Y-axis column name.
 6941:   series   string  Optional.  Categorical colour-dimension column.
 6942:   size     string  Optional.  Numeric bubble-radius column.
 6943:   scheme   string  Optional.  One of: tableau10 category10 set2 set3 paired.
 6944:   min_r    int     Optional.  Minimum radius 1-40 (default 3).
 6945:   max_r    int     Optional.  Maximum radius 1-40 (default 15).
 6946:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 6947: 
 6948: =head4 OUTPUT
 6949: 
 6950:   200 text/html    Rendered scatter chart page.
 6951:   400 text/plain   Missing x= or y= parameter.
 6952:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 6953:   400 text/plain   Unknown colour scheme: <scheme>  (only when series= is set).
 6954:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 6955: 
 6956: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 6957: 
 6958:   scatter_view == lambda self .
 6959:     pre  length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 6960:          /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 6961:          /\ (series_col != '' => series_col in columns)
 6962:          /\ (size_col   != '' => size_col   in columns)
 6963:          /\ (series_col != '' => scheme in SCATTER_SCHEMES)
 6964:          /\ 1 <= min_r <= 40 /\ 1 <= max_r <= 40
 6965:     post render(scatter, pairs)
 6966: 
 6967: =head3 EXAMPLE
 6968: 
 6969:   GET /scatter?l=table:sales&x=quantity&y=amount&series=region
 6970:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 scatter chart SVG
 6971: 
 6972: =cut
 6973: 
 6974: =head2 stacked_view
 6975: 
 6976: C<GET /stacked> -- Render a D3.js stacked bar chart.
 6977: 
 6978: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 6979: 
 6980:   cat=<col>     X-axis category column (required)
 6981:   series=<col>  Stack-segment column (required; seg= is a backwards-compat alias)
 6982:   val=<col>     Numeric measure column (optional; count rows when absent)
 6983:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 6984:   back=<url>    URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 6985: 
 6986: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 6987: 
 6988: =head4 INPUT
 6989: 
 6990:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 6991:   cat     string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name.
 6992:   series  string  Required.  Stack-segment categorical column name.
 6993:   val     string  Optional.  Numeric measure column; omit for count mode.
 6994:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 6995:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 6996: 
 6997: =head4 OUTPUT
 6998: 
 6999:   200 text/html    Rendered stacked bar chart page.
 7000:   400 text/plain   Missing cat= or series= parameter.
 7001:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7002:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7003: 
 7004: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7005: 
 7006:   stacked_view == lambda self .
 7007:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(series_col) > 0
 7008:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ series_col in columns
 7009:     let rows = _grouped_totals(records, cat_col, series_col, val_col) in
 7010:     post render(stacked, rows)
 7011: 
 7012: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7013: 
 7014:   GET /stacked?l=table:sales&cat=region&series=product&val=amount
 7015:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 stacked bar chart SVG
 7016: 
 7017: =cut
 7018: 
 7019: =head2 combo_view
 7020: 
 7021: C<GET /combo> -- Render a D3.js combo bar+line chart.
 7022: 
 7023: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7024: 
 7025:   cat=<col>       X-axis category column (required; x= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7026:   bar_val=<col>   Numeric column plotted as bars (required)
 7027:   line_val=<col>  Numeric column plotted as a line (required)
 7028:   back=<url>      URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7029: 
 7030: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7031: 
 7032: =head4 INPUT
 7033: 
 7034:   l         spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7035:   cat       string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name (x= also accepted).
 7036:   bar_val   string  Required.  Numeric bar-measure column.
 7037:   line_val  string  Required.  Numeric line-measure column.
 7038:   back      url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7039: 
 7040: =head4 OUTPUT
 7041: 
 7042:   200 text/html    Rendered combo chart page.
 7043:   400 text/plain   Missing cat=, bar_val=, or line_val= parameter.
 7044:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7045:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7046: 
 7047: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7048: 
 7049:   combo_view == lambda self .
 7050:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(bar_col) > 0 /\ length(line_col) > 0
 7051:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ bar_col in columns /\ line_col in columns
 7052:     post render(combo, pairs)
 7053: 
 7054: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7055: 
 7056:   GET /combo?l=table:sales&cat=month&bar_val=revenue&line_val=units
 7057:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 combo bar+line chart SVG
 7058: 
 7059: =cut
 7060: 
 7061: =head2 treemap_view
 7062: 
 7063: C<GET /treemap> -- Render a D3.js treemap where tile area encodes a numeric column.
 7064: 
 7065: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7066: 
 7067:   group=<col>  Tile-label categorical column (required; cat= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7068:   size=<col>   Tile-area numeric column (required; val= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7069:   topn=N       Keep only the top-N tiles by size; remainder collapsed into "Other"
 7070:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 7071:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7072: 
 7073: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7074: 
 7075: =head4 INPUT
 7076: 
 7077:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7078:   group   string  Required.  Tile-label categorical column (cat= also accepted).
 7079:   size    string  Required.  Tile-area numeric column (val= also accepted).
 7080:   topn    int     Optional.  Keep top-N tiles; 0 or absent means no limit.
 7081:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 7082:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7083: 
 7084: =head4 OUTPUT
 7085: 
 7086:   200 text/html    Rendered treemap page.
 7087:   400 text/plain   Missing group= or size= parameter.
 7088:   400 text/plain   Unrecognised scheme= parameter (?color= returns 400).
 7089:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7090:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7091: 
 7092: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7093: 
 7094:   treemap_view == lambda self .
 7095:     pre  length(group_col) > 0 /\ length(size_col) > 0
 7096:          /\ group_col in columns /\ size_col in columns
 7097:          /\ color param absent (scheme= is the correct param name)
 7098:     let tiles = _grouped_totals(records, group_col, [], size_sum) in
 7099:     let top   = topn > 0 ? top_n(tiles, topn) : tiles in
 7100:     post render(treemap, top)
 7101: 
 7102: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7103: 
 7104:   GET /treemap?l=table:sales&group=product&size=amount&topn=10
 7105:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 treemap SVG
 7106: 
 7107: =cut
 7108: 
 7109: =head2 histogram_view
 7110: 
 7111: C<GET /histogram> -- Render a D3.js histogram with server-side binning.
 7112: 
 7113: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7114: 
 7115:   col=<col>   Numeric column to bin (required)
 7116:   bins=N      Number of bins (default 10; max 200)
 7117:   back=<url>  URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7118: 
 7119: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7120: 
 7121: =head4 INPUT
 7122: 
 7123:   l     spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7124:   col   string  Required.  Numeric column to histogram.
 7125:   bins  int     Optional.  Bin count 1-200 (default 10).
 7126:   back  url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7127: 
 7128: =head4 OUTPUT
 7129: 
 7130:   200 text/html    Rendered histogram page.
 7131:   400 text/plain   Missing col= parameter.
 7132:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7133:   200 text/html    Empty histogram page when no numeric values exist.
 7134:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7135: 
 7136: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7137: 
 7138:   histogram_view == lambda self .
 7139:     pre  length(col) > 0 /\ col in columns
 7140:          /\ 1 <= bins <= 200
 7141:     let vals  = { row[col] | row in records, looks_like_number(row[col]) } in
 7142:     let width = (max(vals) - min(vals)) / bins in
 7143:     let bdata = bin(vals, width) in
 7144:     post render(histogram, bdata, bins => |bdata|)
 7145: 
 7146: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7147: 
 7148:   GET /histogram?l=table:sales&col=amount&bins=20
 7149:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 histogram SVG
 7150: 
 7151: =cut
 7152: 
 7153: =head2 calc_list / calc_save / calc_delete
 7154: 
 7155: C<GET /calc/list>, C<POST /calc/save>, C<POST /calc/delete> -- Manage stored
 7156: calculated fields for a table spec.
 7157: 
 7158: Calculated fields are named expression strings evaluated per-row by
 7159: C<Database::BI::Calc> and appended as virtual columns before any aggregation.
 7160: 
 7161: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7162: 
 7163: =head4 calc_list (GET /calc/list)
 7164: 
 7165:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7166:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "fields": { "<name>": "<expr>", ... } }
 7167:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7168: 
 7169: =head4 calc_save (POST /calc/save)
 7170: 
 7171:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7172:           name=<id>  (required; must match /\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/)
 7173:           expr=<expression>  (required; max 512 chars; hostile patterns rejected)
 7174:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7175:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }  (compile error or hostile input)
 7176: 
 7177: =head4 calc_delete (POST /calc/delete)
 7178: 
 7179:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7180:           name=<id>  (required)
 7181:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7182:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7183: 
 7184: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7185: 
 7186:   POST /calc/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&name=margin&expr=amount-cost
 7187:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7188:   GET /calc/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7189:     -> 200 { "fields": { "margin": "amount-cost" } }
 7190: 
 7191: =cut
 7192: 
 7193: =head2 groups_list / groups_save / groups_delete
 7194: 
 7195: C<GET /groups/list>, C<POST /groups/save>, C<POST /groups/delete> -- Manage
 7196: named value groups that produce virtual C<${col}_group> columns.
 7197: 
 7198: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7199: 
 7200: =head4 groups_list (GET /groups/list)
 7201: 
 7202:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required), col=<col>  (required)
 7203:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "groups": { "<label>": ["<val>", ...], ... } }
 7204:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" } or "col= required"
 7205: 
 7206: =head4 groups_save (POST /groups/save)
 7207: 
 7208:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>, values=<newline-separated values>
 7209:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7210:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }
 7211: 
 7212: =head4 groups_delete (POST /groups/delete)
 7213: 
 7214:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>  (omit group= to delete all groups for col)
 7215:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7216:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7217: 
 7218: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7219: 
 7220:   POST /groups/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region&group=North&values=MN%0AWI%0AIA
 7221:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7222:   GET /groups/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region
 7223:     -> 200 { "groups": { "North": ["MN","WI","IA"] } }
 7224: 
 7225: =cut
 7226: 
 7227: =head2 dates_list / dates_save / dates_delete
 7228: 
 7229: C<GET /dates/list>, C<POST /dates/save>, C<POST /dates/delete> -- Manage stored
 7230: date-normalisation patterns that convert non-ISO date columns to ISO-8601
 7231: (C<YYYY-MM-DD>) in-place before aggregation.
 7232: 
 7233: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7234: 
 7235: =head4 dates_list (GET /dates/list)
 7236: 
 7237:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required)
 7238:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "patterns": { "<col>": "<pattern>", ... } }
 7239:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7240: 
 7241: =head4 dates_save (POST /dates/save)
 7242: 
 7243:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, pattern=<fmt>
 7244:           Supported patterns: %m/%d/%Y  %d/%m/%Y  %d-%b-%Y
 7245:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "ok": 1 }
 7246:           400 application/json  { "error": "Unsupported date format pattern ..." }
 7247: 
 7248: =head4 dates_delete (POST /dates/delete)
 7249: 
 7250:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>  (omit col= to delete all patterns for the spec)
 7251:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7252:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7253: 
 7254: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7255: 
 7256:   POST /dates/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=sale_date&pattern=%m/%d/%Y
 7257:     -> 200 { "ok": 1 }
 7258:   GET /dates/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7259:     -> 200 { "patterns": { "sale_date": "%m/%d/%Y" } }
 7260: 
 7261: =cut
 7262: 
 7263: =head1 DESCRIPTION
 7264: 
 7265: All user-facing routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.
 7266: See the individual action POD above for per-endpoint documentation.
 7267: 
 7268: =head2 Filter operators and the quick-filter bar
 7269: 
 7270: Filters are expressed as C<f=col:op:val> query parameters and are applied
 7271: server-side after all joins.  Multiple C<f=> params are applied in order
 7272: (AND semantics).
 7273: 
 7274: The colon separator is split with a limit of 3, so values may themselves
 7275: contain colons (e.g. C<f=sale_date:eq:2025-01-15>, or a time value like
 7276: C<f=start_time:eq:14:30:00>).
 7277: 
 7278: B<Supported operators:>
 7279: 
 7280:   eq        case-insensitive string equality
 7281:   ne        case-insensitive string inequality
 7282:   contains  case-insensitive substring match
 7283:   starts    case-insensitive prefix match
 7284:   lt        numeric less-than
 7285:   le        numeric less-than-or-equal
 7286:   gt        numeric greater-than
 7287:   ge        numeric greater-than-or-equal
 7288:   empty     cell is undef or empty string (val ignored)
 7289:   notempty  cell is defined and non-empty (val ignored)
 7290: 
 7291: B<Quick-filter bar (table view):>
 7292: 
 7293: A filter row below the toolbar lets the user pick a column, operator, and
 7294: value and press "Apply filter" (or Enter) to add a condition without
 7295: opening any panel.  The current URL is updated with the new C<f=> param
 7296: and the page reloads; all other URL parameters (joins, dedup, etc.) are
 7297: preserved automatically via the browser C<URL> API.
 7298: 
 7299: Active filter chips appear in the toolbar.  Each chip has an individual
 7300: x-button to remove just that condition.  The "x Clear filters" link
 7301: removes all C<f=> params at once.
 7302: 
 7303: B<Drill-down from pie chart:>
 7304: 
 7305: Clicking a pie slice or legend entry on C</pie> navigates to the table
 7306: view with C<f=cat_col:eq:label> appended, so the user can inspect the
 7307: rows behind any slice without manually typing a filter.
 7308: 
 7309: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 7310: 
 7311: These are the most common mistakes when working with this controller.
 7312: 
 7313: =over 4
 7314: 
 7315: =item B<SQLite files must use the .sql extension, not .sqlite>
 7316: 
 7317: C<Database::Abstraction> (the data-reading library) probes for a file called
 7318: C<tablename.sql> when it wants to open a SQLite database.  It does B<not>
 7319: look for C<.sqlite> or C<.db3>.  If your file is called C<inventory.sqlite>,
 7320: rename it to C<inventory.sql> or it will not appear in the file browser and
 7321: will return 404 when opened.
 7322: 
 7323: =item B<Filter values that contain a colon still work>
 7324: 
 7325: A date like C<2025-01-15> contains hyphens, not colons, so it is fine.  But
 7326: if your value itself contains a colon (for example, a time like C<14:30:00>),
 7327: the filter still works because the C<col:op:val> spec is split on the B<first
 7328: two> colons only -- the rest of the string becomes the value.
 7329: 
 7330:   # This correctly matches "14:30:00" in the start_time column:
 7331:   ?f=start_time:eq:14:30:00
 7332: 
 7333: =item B<Left join keeps only the FIRST matching right-table row>
 7334: 
 7335: When the right table has two rows with the same join key, only the first one
 7336: (in file order) is used.  The second is silently ignored.  If you need all
 7337: matches, consider pre-processing your data so join keys are unique.
 7338: 
 7339: =item B<Export format comes from the filename extension, not a Content-Type header>
 7340: 
 7341: When using C<POST /export> to save a file to disk, the format (CSV or SQLite)
 7342: is determined by the extension of the C<filename> parameter.  C<.csv> produces
 7343: a CSV file; C<.sql> produces a SQLite database.  Any other extension returns
 7344: HTTP 415 (Unsupported Media Type).  The C<Content-Type> request header is
 7345: ignored entirely.
 7346: 
 7347: =item B<Template Toolkit variables starting with underscore are silently dropped>
 7348: 
 7349: If you add a stash variable with a name starting with C<_> (for example,
 7350: C<_tmp> or C<_result>), Template Toolkit will silently produce an empty string
 7351: when the template tries to read it.  This is a TT quirk when C<TRIM =E<gt> 1>
 7352: is active.  Always use names that start with a letter.
 7353: 
 7354: =item B<_apply_filter_spec always passes all records through for unknown operators>
 7355: 
 7356: If you pass an operator that is not in the supported list (for example C<regex>
 7357: or C<like>), the filter is treated as a no-op and B<all rows are returned>.  No
 7358: error is produced.  This is intentional so that future operators can be added
 7359: without breaking existing clients that read a wider response.
 7360: 
 7361: =item B<Uploaded files are evicted automatically, but not immediately>
 7362: 
 7363: Files uploaded via C<POST /upload> are stored in C<.uploads/> under the
 7364: application home directory.  C<Database::BI> evicts upload subdirectories
 7365: whose modification time is older than 24 hours B<on every server startup>.
 7366: Between restarts, files accumulate.  For an immediate full purge (regardless
 7367: of age), post to C<POST /uploads/clear> or click the "Clear upload cache"
 7368: button in the UI.
 7369: 
 7370: =item B<Open C<data/> tables by name; open other files by absolute path>
 7371: 
 7372: The C<view> action (C<GET /view/:table>) only looks inside C<data_dir>.
 7373: To open a file from anywhere else on the filesystem, use C<open_file>
 7374: (C<GET /open?path=/abs/path>).  The two routes use different URL schemes and
 7375: are not interchangeable.
 7376: 
 7377: =back
 7378: 
 7379: =head1 LIMITATIONS
 7380: 
 7381: =over 4
 7382: 
 7383: =item *
 7384: 
 7385: Multi-table joins are delegated to C<Database::Join> with C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>.
 7386: For datasets up to C<join_max_rows> combined rows (default 10,000; configurable
 7387: in C<database_bi.conf>) the join runs in Perl memory; larger datasets spill to
 7388: a temporary SQLite database so peak RAM is bounded by the result set rather than
 7389: the sum of all source tables.
 7390: 
 7391: =item *
 7392: 
 7393: C<_resolve_language> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 7394: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 7395: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 7396: 
 7397: =item *
 7398: 
 7399: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 7400: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 7401: 
 7402: =item *
 7403: 
 7404: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 7405: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 7406: is emitted and the restriction is not enforced in that context.
 7407: 
 7408: =back
